Release Notes\n\ngraphql-java/graphql-java\n\n### [`v17.0`](https://togithub.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/releases/v17.0)\n\n[Compare Source](https://togithub.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/compare/v16.2...v17.0)\n\nWe are happy to announce v17.0 of graphql-java\n\n[https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/milestone/31?closed=1](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/milestone/31?closed=1)\n\n### `@deprecated` supported on input fields\n\nIn line with coming graphql spec changes, the `@deprecated` annotation can now be placed on input fields and not just output fields as before.\n\n[graphql-java/graphql-java#2186](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/pull/2186)\n\n### Dramatic performance improvements in `GraphQLSchema` building\n\nThe `GraphQLSchema` is a graph of types and as such has to be built according to strict rules. The previous code would repeated visit and rebuilt the schema and this meant that large schemes could be slow to build.\n\nThis code has been extensively benchmarked and improved. For example on a very large and complex schema, total build time went from 3.5 seconds to 1.2 seconds.\n\nThank you to teams out there that used [https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-anonymizer](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-anonymizer) to submit in real life schemas that we can test with.\n\n### Dramatic performance improvements in `DataFetchingFieldSelectionSet`\n\nThe classes that underlie the `DataFetchingFieldSelectionSet` code have been been remodelled to be dramatically more performant. Before interfaces could create an exponential explosion in objects created. This has been fixed which in turn improves the performance of this code as scale.\n\n### Dramatic performance improvements in large query validation\n\nThe graphql spec calls for overlapping fields in fragments to be validated in a certain way. The previous code followed the specs psudeo-algorithm correctly but it leads to asymptotic performance problems.\n\nThis has been rewritten via [graphql-java/graphql-java#2495](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/pull/2495) based on this article [https://tech.xing.com/graphql-overlapping-fields-can-be-merged-fast-ea6e92e0a01](https://tech.xing.com/graphql-overlapping-fields-can-be-merged-fast-ea6e92e0a01) which dramatically improves how fast large queries can validated.\n\n### Support of directives on fields and types during introspection\n\n[#​2221](https://togithub.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/issues/2221) this is a non spec feature which lets you expose directives through Introspection\n\n### Reworked default value validation and improved Scalar coercion\n\n[#​2325](https://togithub.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/issues/2325) This is a relatively big change which fixed up how default values are processed. Before that default values were never validated and custom Scalars could cause invalid values printed in SDL or Introspection. This PR includes a new Scalar coercion function `valueToLiteral` which is needed to implement this correct behaviour.\n\n### Better Unicode support in documents\n\n[graphql-java/graphql-java#2404](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/pull/2404) allows for better Unicode expression in queries and SDL documents\n\n### GraphQLContext is now the approved context mechanism\n\n[graphql-java/graphql-java#2368](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/pull/2368) allows for a well known API for context within a graphql request. Previously any opaque object could be used and framework could was not able to make any assumptions about adding their own context properties\n\nThe old mechanism has been deprecated and a singleton (buy mutable) context object is now how context is passed around.\n\n### Ignored characters are no longer help in memory by default\n\nCharacters in a graphql document that are not significant to the syntax, such as spaces, new lines and comms, used to be captured in memory against AST nodes.\n\nThis is no longer the case and it saves memory from parsed documents. There is a switch to turn on the old behavior intended for tooling code.\n\n[graphql-java/graphql-java#2394](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/pull/2394)\n\n### Exception handlers can now be asynchronous\n\ngraphql-java operates in a asynchronous world and hence the code that handles exceptions in data fetchers should itself allow asynchronous handling.\n\n[graphql-java/graphql-java#2371](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/pull/2371)\n\n### Breaking changes\n\n[https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/pulls?q=is%3Apr+label%3A%22breaking+change%22++milestone%3A%2217.0%22](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/pulls?q=is%3Apr+label%3A%22breaking+change%22++milestone%3A%2217.0%22)\n\n-   The boolean values acceptable as variable input the Boolean scalar have been tightened to `true` and `false` - see [Strict boolean support for Strings in and out graphql-java/graphql-java#2419](https://github.com/graphql-java/graphql-java/pull/2419)\n    \n-   The non standard `@Fetch` directive support has been removed by default and deprecated. This was done for performance reasons because very few people used it but it slowed down schema creation a lot\n    \n-   The long deprecated `BatchedExecutionStrategy` and `ExecutorServiceExecutionStrategy` have finally been removed\n    \n\n---\n\n### Configuration\n\n📅 **Schedule**: At any time (no schedule defined).\n\n🚦 **Automerge**: Disabled by config. Please merge this manually once you are satisfied.\n\n♻ **Rebasing**: Renovate will not automatically rebase this PR, because other commits have been found.\n\n🔕 **Ignore**: Close this PR and you won't be reminded about this update again.\n\n---\n\n-    If you want to rebase/retry this PR, check this box.\n\n---\n\nThis PR has been generated by [WhiteSource Renovate](https://renovate.whitesourcesoftware.com).
